It is also necessary to introduce a sanctions mechanism, which we will be introducing today in an amendment. It was not the stupid opposition that said that a law, a constitutional law without sanction is not worth anything, but it was the President of the Constitutional Court, ladies and gentlemen, namely Dr. Holzinger, who said that. He said when you presented this: That's nothing worth it, which leads to uncertainty. That's harmful, he said! Ladies and gentlemen, also from the media: It is not the opposition here that is not responsible, but it is the Federal Government that is evading the responsibility of a national consensus, because one does not even take seriously the council of the Constitutional Court and incorporates this sanctions mechanism into the law. [[BZÖ's condolences.]] Ladies and gentlemen of SPÖ and ÖVP, perhaps And then you too. Their negotiations lead to this. If you look at this amendment, there is the inside: Sanctions mechanism, exit from the debt limit only with a two-thirds majority of the National Council, a ceiling on taxes and taxes. That's really a signal to the markets and to the public that we're serious. We are also willing to go along with you on the path to savings. The proposals are on the table: The Court of Auditors has made them; we have a number of requests here in the House. Let us sit together together and define this path, but not through the way of new taxes – for the taxpayers in Austria are broken enough – but through concrete savings measures! We will also implement these when you are ready to do so. But, unfortunately, hope has already become very weak here, because you can't even overcome the opposition of the institutions in the smallest areas – see now also civil servants' union or countries. I would have another concrete proposal, Mr Bundeskanzler or Mrs Finanzminister. After all, we have the problem that our government debt is 75 percent abroad; to 75 percent, there has been some reverse in recent years, it has also been quite different. This means that the Austrians are too little invested in the government's own bonds, often because they do not know that there is this possibility. Instead, the savings of the Austrians are badly interested in savings books from the Austrian banks, and the banks take this money in order to speculate it again abroad. Or the pension funds: The pension funds invest the money of the Austrians not in Austrian state bonds, where they are safe, where they also have a return, but also in stock funds and other things abroad, and we now see that the supplementary pensions are reduced. Well, wouldn't that be a national action, Mrs. Finanzminister? – The Austrians would be willing to do this because they would also have an advantage of being in solidarity here, because some of the money that is badly paid on savings books will be invested in Austrian bonds, which currently account for more than 3 percent, 3.7 percent. [[BZÖ's condolences.]] Then they would be in debt in Austria, the Austrians would have more percent for the money they contribute accordingly, and our dependence on rating agencies, on foreign hedge funds and others that dictate the interest rates would be less. As is the case in Japan: The Japanese are 200 percent in debt, much more than most euro countries. But there are no problems with rating agencies and with other things at all, because the money has been taken up almost 100 percent in their own country, because their own population is investing their money in the state. That would be an idea – Mrs Finanzminister, if you would listen here when a Member speaks – that you do not increase taxes, that you do not argue that you are not putting in place a placebos here, but that you can also take the money in Austria, which you need to clean up the budget.

Today's debate, which has been held very intensively – not only today, but also in the Constitutional Committee, in the media, in various discussions – is an important and crucial one for Austria. The debt brake is not about a political project of this federal government, it is not a political commitment, it is about the future of Austria in the form of a clear, unmistakable signal to financial markets, which are currently making problems for the states and for the financing of the states. [[Abg. Dr. Moser: In Germany she does not use anything!]] This clear signal that we can or should hope for is, of course, best to be given with a debt brake in the constitutional order. Westenthaler: If you make a tax brake, we'll be there!]] But I think it's a project that you can't put ad acta, but still have to follow. And if we decide on a simple legal debt brake, we should also be aware that it is considered as if it had been decided in the constitutional order. The advantage of the debt brake is also that it means ensuring structurally balanced households in the medium term in Austria, including with the involvement of all local authorities. This is a particularly important point because it is about creating more transparency, greater comparability in the individual budgets of Austria and ensuring stability as a whole. By the way, we would have succeeded in securing the Stability Pact and all these measures with constitutional majority, but also with Article 15a agreements it is possible and it should or should be the next project, at least these basic principles to continue to be anchored. The guiding principles in the design of the present draft are debt reduction, sustainability of public finances, balancing economic fluctuations by automatic stabilizers – also possible and desirable in the future – and, of course, the potential flexibility in the fight against crisis itself. But, and that's the important point: Fiscal discipline will in future play the most important role not only in Austria – in the Federation, in the countries and in the communities – but also in Europe as a whole, in order to regain the political scope for action for those political projects that concern us; the scope for action that has been lost in the crisis. The recovery of scope for action also means to me that reducing a budget deficit means reducing the distance between expenditure and revenue. This means that I need to talk about both expenditure-side measures, structural improvements and savings, and revenue-side measures, structural improvements in the tax system. The best is those measures which do not dampen economic growth and do not negatively affect it. That is also a very important point. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Let me also say the following: It is an important point, both for reasons of justice and for reasons of revenue strategy, because only with economic growth will we be able to advance the fight against debt as we imagine it to be. It is also not to be found that, for me and for the Social Democratic Group, taxes related to property are, of course, not only socially fair, but also economically meaningful, because they have little impact on growth. But all these questions are not debated today, but the next financial framework and the next ones. Today it's about making this clear: Austria commits itself to fiscal discipline at all political levels! And it's a second thing to make clear: If we do what we call debt brakes, we are at the same time fighting with all the strength that the financial markets will be regulated even more and more effectively in all areas, for the simple reason that those who have caused this crisis, namely unregulated financial markets, will be prevented from bringing us back into such an economic crisis in the future. In this sense, I also thank you for the constructive discussions with parts of the opposition, even though it unfortunately did not lead to an agreement that disappointed me, because some opposition politicians, especially the BZÖ, in fact also very often demanded the debt brake for German model – that is in about what is present today, namely a strong orientation on the German model – so that I would have expected from the point of view that the two-thirds majority is also present today. But no matter how, the clear signal to all markets is: Austria will go into a future of stable and balanced, structurally balanced state finances!

ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-07-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00137_d8e13419	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Mr. State Secretary! High house! Today, we are dealing again with a chapter with the great heading of anticorruption and transparency, namely a Federal Constitutional Act on the transparency of media cooperation as well as of advertising contracts and promotions to media owners of a periodic medium. What does that mean? – It is about that the legal authorities controlled by the Court of Auditors have disclosed their media cooperation and advertising contracts and that this is done with details. In other words, there must be information about the media companies and their ownership, and I am pleased to say that the real property relationship must be. This means that there must also be disclosure of trust and similar conditions and that details of the orders themselves are also announced. The grants must be announced and the disclosure made to the Kommunestria. In addition, something is to come that is also very pleasing, namely a ban on advertising for ministries, as it is so beautiful in the legislative text, without “concrete reference to covering an information need”. That is, until now, it has apparently been possible to make an intervention and use public money without any concrete reference to covering a need for information. So we are already very glad that one has come up with it and now wants to prevent and can prevent it with this law. [[Abg. Transparency creates trust, transparency is public control and public debate, and I think it is a right and good way that we have worked together here. There are also some things that we can take part in this law, namely a clear expansion of the disclosure of ownership relations between media and media owners, because, in the meantime, the competition authority is on its way, there may be antitrust problems due to certain quellings that are suspected, but otherwise there could be ruptures with policies that are certainly not desirable. With this law, we want to make a substantial contribution to the fact that journalism in our country can take place independently and unaffected by the other economic interests of the same company – i.e. in the media company – that journalists in that country can exercise their task fully independently and, last but not least, for the benefit of democracy in that country. To this end, this law will make a substantial contribution. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, I know it does not have all the pleasure with the very strict provisions and conditions in this law. But I call on all those affected by it – in the most diverse ministries and government agencies – to take a brief look back in the light of this law and to ask themselves where the continuation of this expansive acceleration of reporting and economic interests would lead to the end. It would harm our democracy, and we cannot all want to do so. That's why I'm looking for everyone in this high house, This very good, very far-reaching law, in the sense of maintaining and functioning our democracy, is to be approved. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-07-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00137_d8e13638	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! To my surprise and to my delight, the media transparency law that we are going to decide today really deserves the name. I think there were several approaches to explain why you need this thing. The first point was – and this was established by the Association of Austrian Newspapers – that there was a different weighting on the question of government communication. When you look back, you have to ask the question: What extent should government communication actually take in the area of political communication? I believe that the relationship that has developed in recent years between public media promotion and the extent of what has been awarded to advertising and advertising by government agencies is now problematic. Since 2000, I believe that we have had a significant increase in the number of ads: for example, factor 1: 3. I believe that if you sum up all the ministries – I have the Federal Chancellery in my head with a budget of €4.8 million for the next year – over all ministries, then next year you will receive an advertising volume of €30 million. That's about three times what's in the press promotion. In principle, democratic issues should be considered whether strengthening press promotion would be of no use and whether, if one considers that the media landscape should be promoted, criteria should be used. But what has now succeeded is that we have not only the disclosure of the owners – that has already been mentioned; this is also a point that is relevant to creating transparency – that we know the order of magnitude – whereby we must say that some things have been more transparent until now than they have been perceived; because of parliamentary questions, at least the volume of ministries was known, in this area, more clearly known than will be the case now, because there have been individual publications there, that is, one could understand de facto which advertisements have been made at what price; now, on the other hand, there is a time period that has been published. However, there will be much greater transparency in the area of government agencies or close-to-state companies. This is very pleasant and should come. The historic hour was then debated as well as advertising and their design, so that there was a real opportunity to identify the criteria for content. I think that is actually the cultural revolution in this country. I have had several discussions with the Association of Advertising Industries in recent days. Funnyly, there was no resistance there, even to this head ban question. They said – and I think that was a very good argument – that: What has been advertised on government communications in recent years was not campaigns, but there was the wish: I want my photo in the paper; can you write me a text for it? – This has nothing to do with information policy! If you take seriously that €30 million can be used for government communications, there must also be the claim that this really will move something. There are topics on which you can talk. Some examples of this have always been mentioned positively, including from the transport resort this belt campaign, where it has really been a content where it is a matter of producing something. That was quite different than turning an advertisement like – that was one of the last things I remember –: “Government Faymann stops the two-class medicine.” That was then passed through, where the content does not even have anything to do with reality, let alone an information behind it. Now it is about being able to make information campaigns, without the Minister's photo and without a name designation, but with a content. The great cultural revolution is that this also applies to the countries. As a Lower Austrian, I can still imagine that the “NÖN” ads will be read by the Lower Austrian government, where not a significant head will be advertised with a hairstyle similar to that of my colleague Green Forest. It's really a cultural revolution in this country that won't happen in every newspaper; and I think that's something that really justifies the concept of media transparency law. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] [[Abg. Dr. Wittmann: Probably only the hairstyle will come!]] - That's an interesting point. If you let go of your head and just get the hairstyle, you'll still recognize it. Let's see if there's something coming. A wormhead drop is that it has not succeeded in negotiating that the parties would also have been obliged to publish their advertisement. We had tried this, for a simple reason: There is not only the rumor in the industry, but many indications that ad circuits have also worked like this: Government agencies have advertised. We know there are discounts on ads. These discounts have not been used. Instead, it has been said that the discounts come into another pot, that they can be used – and that is de facto a detour of party funding! We would, of course, have needed the disclosure of the Party Council. That's not what happened. However, we have a parliamentary committee of inquiry, where advertising is on the agenda. We will address the question of the extent to which cuts or discounts have not been used by ministries and close-to-state bodies and may have been made to parties by kick-back payments. In this respect, the Committee of Inquiry has a task, in spite of this law, which should be a clear progress in Austrian political culture.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-07-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00137_d8e13869	Ladies and gentlemen! Mr President-in-Office and Secretary-General of the VöZ! I now refrain from actually correcting the actual correction and will talk about the media transparency law. ==References== In June in the Federal Government, a decision on a media transparency law was taken after we had carried out a very thorough review process before that. Since then, the discussion has lasted a little bit, but it was very useful. Contrary to the original intention to make transparency rules only for the Federal Government, we felt that it was wise to light not only part of the cake, but the whole cake. We have therefore proposed, and then in the government too, that it should be decided as a Constitutional Act that all the institutions examined by the Court of Auditors should be included – in other words, the Federal Government, the Länder, the Communities, which are audited by the Court of Auditors, and companies which are audited by the Court of Auditors. We have proposed that the place where the whole is to be located, that all this should be reported, that the Community should be constitutionally independent in the previous year. We have been looking for a way that creates as much transparency as possible on the one hand, but on the other hand we also avoid additional excessive administrative burden. The path we have taken after the intensive discussion is such. I believe that we have actually found the narrow grat that exists: on the one hand, creating transparency, on the other, the goal of reforming administration, reducing administration, not losing sight of it. We therefore decided in the discussion not to settle the issue of the disclosure of ownership in the media transparency law – I also held a discussion with the Minister of Justice, in which we discussed it – but to settle it where it is already regulated, namely in § 25 media law, to extend it accordingly and to dock it there. I think that's a legitimately clean way. We have refrained from implementing a proposal, which has also been put forward, namely that the Kommunestria will examine monthly whether all the ads comply with the directives, because this would have led to a red tape sample. We have taken the decision that transparency should be used to control this issue. The advertisement is switched on and is therefore also controlled accordingly. Last week we took into account a concern expressed by the President of the Court of Auditors. He said that it could lead to challenges at the Constitutional Court if, at the beginning of the year, he would publish all the institutions that were against the line we were pursuing, namely to relieve the Constitutional Court, and that the Constitutional Court would be taxed in addition. We have therefore also taken this objection into account and made a corresponding scheme which does not affect that which follows this proposal. In total, as I mean, we have found a practical, very sensible law. I would therefore like to express my express thanks to all those who have worked intensively: the staff in the clubs, the staff with us in the cabinet, but above all also the staff in the Constitutional Service, Dr. Kogler, Dr. Hesse, who carried out this very complex legal work. I would like to express my express thanks to all those who go with us today. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-07-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00137_d8e14039	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! It is about how the international agreement OPCAT – namely the protection from torture and degrading and inhumane treatment – is transferred to the Austrian legal order. We have nothing against it, of course, this is an important initiative. We have no objection either that the People's Procuratorate – as is now planned – is entrusted with this task, but we have two criticisms that have prompted us not to agree to this proposal. One is that we believe that this agreement has been implemented relatively complicatedly. It is transferred to the People's Procuratorate to the existing Human Rights Advisory Council. He is then only more advisory body, but is increased from 11 to 30 members. There are a number of commissions to do the actual work with up to 40 people. And the People's Procuratorate itself has defined that it needs 15 additional employees – but we do not yet know how high the work will really be – and they will also be approved accordingly. It is not to be forgotten that the quality requirements for the Chairman of this Human Rights Advisory Council have been changed. So far, he or the chairman has been nominated from the circle of the Supreme Judges. Now, suddenly, only more is required to have knowledge in the areas of human rights and administrative agendas. If you think you have to create a position for one of the prosecutors inside, you might also have to say that, but a “Lex Stoisits” doesn’t necessarily have to be cast so clearly and clearly into law. But the main reason that led us to this rejection is that a chance has been missed, namely the People's Procuratorate – an important institution – on a to set a modern, forward-looking basis. In recent years, there has been a wide range of solicitations in various fields: Disability attorney, pediatric lawyer, animal protection attorney, everything possible. These are important, good institutions, but in total they are designed with little clarity. We would have the proposal that the public prosecutorship should be completely re-ordered, that the order mode should be changed, that the number of lawyers should be increased if the tasks should also be increased: that these entire public prosecutorships should be concentrated – with a proper distribution of tasks – on a concentrated public prosecutorship. Then you can also discuss an extension of the staff level – but only then – because at the other levels you save much resources. It is also optimal to apply for this public prosecutorship as a representation of the citizens' concerns towards the administration and in cooperation with the legislator. Unfortunately, this has not happened, but it is only partial reforms that may bring more bureaucracy than in the past. That is why we reject the proposal as it has been set out here.
ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-07-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00137_d8e14096	Next speaker: Mr Wittmann. 3 minutes speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-07-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00137_d8e14118	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! This law is essentially the implementation of a protocol signed in 2003. It is an agreement to prevent torture and other cruel, inhuman and degrading treatments. The Protocol was signed in 2003 and is already signed on 22. ==References== This Protocol calls on Member States to set up one or more bodies to ensure that such actions no longer take place. In essence, it is also a matter of visiting all places of abandonment – no matter how much they may be called. This is more to be seen as a description now, as it is about prisons, but also about nursing homes, about children's homes, about other facilities to be visited. I think it's good to settle that at the public prosecutor's office, which also has the resources to do so. You can use existing resources here. I also believe that the Human Rights Advisory Council, which has until now partly observed these agendas, is better taken up by the People's Procuratorate than by the Ministry of the Interior. In contrast to my previous speakers, I believe that it is important to demand some basic knowledge in the administrative and human rights fields in order to be able to apply for the People's Procuratorate. That is, as I mean, a very tangible, positive law. From 1 000 facilities to date, the possibility of visitation is extended to some 4,000 facilities. The need for a number of commissions – that is, six – and an extended staff in the public prosecutor's office is clear. I believe that this institution is perfectly located at the public prosecutor's office and that it can work efficiently. Finally, I believe that we are following international agreements.

With this item on the agenda, we are now going back to a good example of the red-black propor or actually the red-red-black proporz when we talk about the ORF, or the red-red-red-black-black proporz if you want it that way. But less black than a third or a quarter does not exist at this item on the agenda. [[President Mag. Prammer is taking over the presidency again.]] In the morning the ÖVP, of course also by the SPÖ, spoke at least in terms of savings and debt brakes and the efficiency and protection of citizens from costs and so on. But when it comes to their own proporzklientel, these purposes are quickly swept away and then we are where we are now, with a seemingly small law with a small change, but with great consequences. Instead of being aware of the cost of demanding the discharge of the participants and thus of the citizens, the following is done: The previous principle in the ORF Act was that only those fees that have a device with which you are at least theoretically able to receive the ORF, whether you see it or not, must be paid. You can still see this because others are not controllable. But now it goes so far that even the one who does not have a reception device, who cannot even theoretically see the ORF, has to pay, so also the one who does not have a DVB-T decoding device – I think, that's what it means – can't even see the ORF theoretically. He's got to pay now, too. And there are now the most adventurous logical-juristic flaws in the reasoning. So I have to quote a sentence that stands in there, because I especially like it: This amendment corresponds to ‘the synallagmatic nature of the programme fee in the sense of a consideration for the provision made’ (). [[Rejoicing with representatives of the FPÖ.]] That the citizen does not understand is only good in that case. I understand, of course, as a lawyer. “Synallagmatic” means – and not all will know here – that beautiful Greek word means “to each other” or “to replace”, i.e. an exchange relationship. Here it is assumed that a contractual situation will be remedied – with a contract for the applicants, i.e. for the government parties, if one provides a service. So if Billa opens a business and provides me with a performance, the purchase, then I have to pay for it according to the logic of this law, because the mutual performance is the provision of a service. Anyone who offers something already delivers a performance, I have to pay for it. All this is done to avoid the ugly word that the so-called programme fee is actually a tax. At the latest with today's novel, the fee has come to a pure tax, because if I have to pay something that I definitely can't even refer to, well, then probably the term "tax" or, if you want, also "pay", but no longer the word "pays". My follow-up speakers – Mr Cap, I have seen on the speaker list – will certainly explain to us that it is important to close gaps and protect the ORF, to enable the public-law mission, to make it possible to devalue it and everything possible. A few figures on actual developments. If you remember that the last “programme fee increase, ” as this is so beautiful, was made in 2009 by over 8 percent. In 2010, we decided to grant special assistance to the ORF. [[Abg. Head: 2008 was the last one to be made!]] – The increase entered into force in 2009. [[Abg. Head: 1.6.2008!]] – Entry into force? - All right, give it to me. 1.6.2008, six months won. However, in 2010 the decision was – we were all there ourselves – at an amount of €150 million to the ORF or €160 million. [[Abg. Head: 160!]] I even called that too little now. Yeah, we fed 160 million euros. That at the time under the premise – I can still remember the speakers –: The ORF has promised, there will be no increase in fees as soon as possible, at the earliest of 2013 you will be able to do that again – go, Petzner! – at the earliest of 2013 you will bring this back to your attention. [[Abg. Grosz: But you have agreed!]] [[Abg. Petzner: You fell on it! You agreed!]] So now, as far as I can remember, we are in December 2011, and next week is already the agenda of the Foundation Council meeting: Programme fee increase by 7 percent. So 2006: 8 percent, 2008: 8 percent, 2010: 160 million €, 2011 with effectiveness 2012: 7 per cent, as my colleague's head says correctly. So that's just a little bit for the dynamics and for the decoupling of the so-called increase. I looked at this a little bit. Overall, the fees of the ORF – I know it because I was in the Foundation Council myself – have grown by an average of 4.1 percent per year over the last 30 years, not only by the increases, but also by the increasing number of visions that there are more and more individual households and therefore more reception equipment. Inflation in the last 30 years – I have not yet taken that into account – has been 2.6 percent. That is, the inflation-adjusted net increase of the ORF, beyond the increase in value, is in the range of 1.5 percent p.a. ==References== Many employees would be happy about this. I do not want to talk at all about the issue of the quality of the programme, the respect of the public service mission, the objectivity, the occupation of the bodies and positions in so-called objective aspects, the post-achievement, the remuneration and so on, but I simply want to conclude by: Of course, to this bill a no.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-07-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00137_d8e16130	Today, we have to take a decision whose need arose from a 2008 ruling by the Administrative Court, which only confirms a fee requirement if the presentation of the ORF programmes with the concrete device constellation is possible. This provides the ORF with the opportunity to actually fulfil its programme mandate and for the quality of its Programmes, especially in the future, will guarantee the financial basis.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-07-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00137_d8e19724	Madam President. Mr. State Secretary! Dear ladies and gentlemen in the High House! Briefly some arguments for our consent, the consent of the BZÖ to the Federal Procurement Act Defense and Security 2012. The security and defence sector will in future be bound by its own EU procurement law. This award law is much more complex than the previous national rules, but it brings some advantages to Austria. This new contract law is particularly useful in the field of national defence. It is known that the Federal Ministry of State Defense has carried out award procedures over the past five years with a total volume of more than € 700 million. According to the Ministry's internal estimates, it can be assumed that in the future, some 80 percent of all resort-related procurements will also take place under the new Federal Procurement Act. Now, ladies and gentlemen, the implementation of the directive is intended to open and strengthen defence and security markets. As a result of the future higher competitive pressure, savings in procurement are expected within the scope of this law. Better competitive opportunities are also created. For example, in the case of standardised services, such as the purchase of ammunition through lower unit prices, costs are significantly reduced. The possibility of cross-border central procurement is also expected to lead to savings potential. There are also easings in the normal allocation area. The administrative burden is greatly reduced. These simplifications will make our small and medium-sized enterprises much easier. These measures benefit domestic companies. Overall, this new law has a positive impact on Austria's economic location. Logically, the BZÖ will agree to the Federal Procurement Act Defense and Security 2012 in particular for security policy considerations.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-07-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00137_d8e21853	I'll call the 18th. Agenda item. First, Mr Mag. Mag. Maier speaking. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2011-12-07-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00137_d8e21877	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! The amendment to the Federal Law on the National Fund of the Republic of Austria for the Victims of National Socialism was unanimously adopted by the Constitutional Committee. In this new law, it is about ensuring an Austrian participation in the renovation and conservation of the international memorial of Auschwitz-Birkenau. High House, the liberation of the concentration camp Auschwitz-Birkenau by the Allies will take place on 27 July. ==References== Year of year. The memorial set up there is the most important international memorial to the crimes of the Shoah in the place of a former extermination camp. Their conservation for the afterworld is therefore of particular importance to Austria in particular. However, the maintenance status of the site has deteriorated significantly in recent years, and its continued existence is at risk without committed reorganisation measures. Austria provides €6 million for the renovation. Off This amount is also intended to cover the Austrian pavilion with the new exhibition. High House, ladies and gentlemen, we must look at these issues of anti-Semitism, legal radicalism and legal terrorism more closely. According to a new study in Germany, 20 percent of the population have latent anti-Semitism. In this study, the right-wing extremist camp is still the most important carrier of anti-Semitism in the Federal Republic. In particular, this finding is underlined by the fact that more than 90 percent of all anti-Semitic crimes are committed by perpetrators associated with the right spectrum. The situation in Austria is similar. High House, Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, I am very grateful to the Archbishop of Salzburg, Kothgasser, for having taken clear positions on the statement of Bishop Laun, who at a commemorative ceremony compared the prohibition of Hitler’s “My Fight” with the burning of books by the National Socialists. To conclude: It should be political command and obligation for all parties represented here in Parliament to combat radicalism, anti-Semitism and xenophobia in all forms, and for the right-wing terrorists who have committed these crimes in Germany to be prosecuted accordingly.

Introduce Johann Maier, colleagues, into the Federal Law amending the Federal Law on the National Fund of the Republic of Austria for the Victims of National Socialism, 1399 of the Supplements. Basel III is concerned with raising capital requirements for banks in the field of lending, as well as with some other provisions, which is good for large international banks. But if you break this down to the small banking sector, as it happens here, then this promotes the credit terminal, then this will lead to credit becoming more expensive for our small entrepreneurs, and we will not vote in any case. With Basel III, no financial crises can be avoided in the future. It's just the credit terminal. It does not matter whether Raiffeisen International is able to do business in Ukraine, because the risk provisions do not comply with the new rules or because equity is not sufficient. It makes no difference for Austria whether this business is done or not; however, it makes a difference whether the Raiffeisenbank in Mistelbach can credit a local craftsmanship or not. That makes a very big difference. That is why we will not agree to this proposal. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] [[Abg. I can only join this in itself, but we have adopted this system in this way, and it has a good reason why it is so complicated: Here, four main types of income – i.e. self-employed income, wages, self-employed income, commercial income and agriculture and forestry – form the basis for all detailed arrangements. They praise on the one hand that the income-dependent child's money is accepted, and we are pleased that the father's participation is accepted and expanded. On the other hand, more and more proposals are being drawn up in the Committee and on other occasions in order to extend this even more widely – for example, freedom of choice after a few months of trial activity, which is a better variant – which entails further control obligations and a more difficult administration. You have me in the boat if you think we should simplify it. I hope for your help. However, the simplification process is also a relatively complicated matter. As far as the contents of the novels now present are concerned, we essentially agreed that there are improvements in most of the points. It was mentioned that the amount of earnings is increased and that there is a flat-rate supplement of 30 percent of the annual profit instead of a supplement of the social security contributions required in the year – and thus also equality between the self-employed and the self-employed. The 24-day rule not only simplifies, but also increases the amount of commitment, and there are also clarifications as to procedural and administrative costs. I do not see why all non-fulfilled obligations of participation should go at the expense of the State. There is a difference as to the clarification of the liability: In the case of a flat-rate child care allowance, the aid remains exempt, the income dependent – because it is income – is subject to the pledge. Last but not least – because the request was made – with regard to the abolition of the earnable limit. We have already talked about this in the morning during Question Time, and several Members have also said the same thing here: In the final effect, the abolition of the earn-out limit would result in the fathers trying to create it as a take-away effect – family policy. Why? - Because they no longer need to reduce working time. This is not intentional, and, Mrs Haubner – she is not here right now – that is not intended in the government programme, but clearly the father's participation in the care work is planned here. That is why we cannot imagine this from the point of view of family politics, but also not from money. This will cost several hundred million if we do so without barriers. Mrs Binder-Maier, you mentioned the matter with the midwives. We had a different information before, I did not have it on the part of the Ministry of Health. Let's take a look at this! If that is the case, we will be happy to make it as clear as you wish. Otherwise, I believe that this is a good regulation, which, in particular, contains improvements. - Thank you many times.

The FPÖ calls for the payment of family allowances to all young people who make a training as a social care provider, whether this training is done in regular full-time training or in a professional training course. We do not want this from the SPÖ, so we reject the FPÖ's request. Thank you.
